L2313: ## 15.6. Modified Fabrazyme Scoring System (mFSS)
L2314: The following text is extracted from the Applicant's Histology Report:
L2315: FSS is semi-quantitative scoring system first introduced by the Fabrazyme® clinical trial, and initially applied using conventional light microscopy. In the FSS scoring method each capillary received a score based on inclusions/granules/aggregates ranging from 0 (no inclusions) to 3 (bulging aggregates). FSS semi-quantitative score is based on the presence, size, and distribution of Gb3 granules in endothelial cells of PTC: 0 = ≤2 granules; 1 = ≥3 granules; 2 = ≥1 non-bulging aggregate; 3 = ≥1 bulging aggregate (Thurberg, et al., 2002).
L2316: For PB-102-F01/F02 studies, the FSS method was modified as followed: a) the FSS was applied to the annotated PTCs from the WSI, b) an overall biopsy score was based on the majority of capillaries with a given score was not implemented and c) the biopsy FSS score was reported as the average number of capillaries scored 0, 0.5, 1, 2, or 3, with 0 representing no deposition and 3 representing severe deposition.
L2317: Source: Applicant's Histology Report, pages 9-10
L2318: ## 15.7. Absence of Spontaneous Reduction in Kidney Gb3
L2319: The Applicant has argued that concerns regarding the single arm design of study PB-102-F01/F02 are mitigated by the lack of evidence for spontaneous decrease of Gb3 concentrations in the kidney. The following text is extracted from the Applicant's Summary of Clinical Efficacy Document:
L2320: By design, a placebo arm was not included in the PB-102-F01/F02 study, since reduction of Gb3 in kidney biopsies is considered an objective measurement of treatment effect (see PB-102-F01/F02 Histology Report). As reported in published placebo-controlled studies with Fabrazyme [Eng 2001; Thurberg 2002] and Galafold [Germain 2016] spontaneous reductions in this biomarker have not been observed in Fabry disease patients, and no improvement in renal Gb3 clearance was observed in the placebo arm. Given the nature of the disease and considering the objectivity of kidney Gb3 accumulation assessment, a spontaneous reduction is not expected.
L2321: Source: Summary of Clinical Efficacy Document, Page 13
L2322: The following text is extracted from the Applicant's Histology Report:
L2323: ## 6.3. Comparison of Pegunigalsidase Alfa to Relevant Published Data with Treated and Untreated Fabry Disease Patients
L2324: In the Phase 3 program for Fabrazyme® (agalsidase beta), kidney PTC in biopsies from previously untreated patients were assessed for Gb3 inclusion bodies at baseline and after 5 months of treatment with either agalsidase beta or placebo, using a semi-quantitative scoring methodology (Eng, et al., 2001; Thurberg, et al., 2002).
L2325: **Figure 15: Kidney Biopsy Data from the Published Phase 3 Development Program for Fabrazyme® (Thurberg et al., 2002)**
L2326: The results from the placebo arm, kidney biopsies demonstrated no spontaneous improvement in the 5 months study period (Figure 15). After 5 months of placebo treatment, all patients were switched to receive agalsidase beta and evaluated at 11 months. The results show that patients treated for 6 months with agalsidase beta after 5 months of placebo treatment phase, resulted with a reduction in Gb3 inclusions, further supporting that Gb3 reduction accrued only following ERT. Regardless of the scoring methodology, these results evidently show that there is no spontaneous reduction in Gb3 burden following placebo treatment.
L2327: The Phase 3 Galafold™ (migalastat) study (AT-1001-11), in which the BLISS quantitative scoring system was used to evaluate Gb3 in kidney PCTs (Figure 16), further demonstrated that placebo patients experienced no reduction in kidney Gb3 after 6 months. Similar to the agalsidase beta results described above, after switching to active treatment (Galafold™) placebo patients had a decline in mean number of Gb3 inclusions per kidney PTC at 6 months from starting active treatment (Germain, et al., 2016).
L2328: **Figure 16: Change from Baseline of Gb3 Inclusions in Kidney PTC in Patients with Mutations That Were Suitable for Migalastat Therapy**
(*Germain et al., 2016*)
L2329: Both studies demonstrated that there is no spontaneous improvement of Gb3 kidney burden in untreated (placebo arm) Fabry patients as assessed by various scoring systems. The patients in the placebo arm of Fabrazyme® and Galafold™ were followed for duration of 5 and 6 months, respectively, a similar period as pegunigalsidase alfa treatment duration in the PB-102-F01/F02 study. This finding was further supported by Fabry disease physician input; therefore, the Sponsor considers that a placebo control arm is not necessary to interpret the results observed for the PB-102-F01/F02 study.
L2330: Source: Applicant's Histology Report, Page 29-33
L2331: From a statistical perspective, the Applicant's rationale for lack of evidence of spontaneous reduction in kidney Gb3 appears reasonable.

L2338: Figure 28: Average Absolute and Percent Change in Plasma Lyso-Gb3 by Sex in Study PB102-F01/F02/F03
L2339: Source: produced by the review team based on the analysis datasets submitted to BLA761161 (eCTD0025) on November 11, 2020
L2340: **Figure 29: Individual Level Distribution of the mFSS Score, Majority-rule Based mFSS Score, Weighted mFSS Score and BLISS Score**
L2341: **mFSS:** modified Fabrazyme Scoring System; **Majority:** Majority rule mFSS score; **Weighted:** weighted mFSS score; The biopsy score for each scoring system is represented using the notation x \to y, where x represents the baseline score, and y represents the six-month score.
L2342: **Source:** produced by the review team based on the analysis datasets submitted to BLA 761161 (eCTD0001) on May 27, 2020
L2343: The above figure shows each patient's Gb3 burden using the semi-quantitative mFSS and quantitative BLISS methodology. In the mFSS, each capillary receives a severity score of 0, 0.5, 1, 2, or 3 and, the proportion of capillaries receiving the given score is calculated. The biopsy-level **weighted mFSS** score is derived by computing the weighted average of the capillary-specific scores. For example, if 30% of capillaries have a score of 3, 49% a score of 2, 20% a score of 1, 10% a score 0.5, and 11% a score of 0, the **weighted mFSS** score will be 2.13 (= 0.3 \times 3 + 0.49 \times 2 + 0.2 \times 1 + 0.1 \times 0.5 + 0.11 \times 0). The biopsy-level **majority-rule mFSS** score corresponds to the score received by the majority of the capillaries. In the above example, the **biopsy-level majority-rule mFSS** score will be 2 since a majority of the capillaries received a score of 2. Compared to the BLISS methodology, the semi-quantitative mFSS is less sensitive to small changes in the number of Gb3 inclusions. For example, the individual shown in the top right panel has a majority-rule score of 0 both at baseline and at six-month, however, the BLISS score for this individual are 0.8 and 0.4 at baseline and six-month, respectively.
L2344: ## 15.9. Evaluation and Treatment Algorithm to monitor and manage hypersensitivity reactions
L2345: During and after infusion of PRX-102, the following algorithm will be followed to monitor and manage the occurrence of hypersensitivity, anaphylaxis, or anaphylactoid reactions.
L2346: ## Clinical signs
L2347: ## Early
L2348: - Sensation of warmth and itching
- Feelings of anxiety
L2349: ## Moderate
L2350: - Pruritus
- Flushing
- Urticaria
- Chest discomfort
- Mild Hypotension
L2351: ## Progressive
L2352: - Erythematous or massive urticarial rash
- Edema of face, neck, soft tissues
L2353: ## Severe
L2354: - Hypotension
- Bronchospasm (wheezing)
- Laryngeal edema (dyspnea, stridor, aphonia, drooling)
- Arrhythmias
L2355: ## Treatment algorithm:
L2356: With the onset of any of the above clinical signs, immediately discontinue study medication administration and initiate the following monitoring.
L2357: - Continuous electrocardiographic monitoring
- Continuous pulse oximetry
- Measure blood pressure every 5 minutes
- Perform chest auscultation every 5 minutes
- Collect blood samples for Tryptase (29-33), antibodies and C3, C4. Tryptase samples need to be withdrawn at:1st sample taken 0.25-3 hours after onset of symptoms
- 2nd sample taken between 3-6 hours
- 3rd sample taken 24-48 hours to verify the return to baseline.
L2358: In the case of progressive or severe hypersensitivity, treat appropriately.
L2359: ## Treat as follows:
L2360: ## Urticaria or edema of the face, neck, or soft tissues
L2361: - Epinephrine 1:1000 solution, 0.5 mL subcutaneously, repeat as needed every 5-10 minutes
- Antihistamines
- Corticosteroids
L2362: ## Hypotension (systolic blood pressure (SBP) \le 90 mmHg)
L2363: - Isotonic sodium chloride solution, 1 L every 30 minutes as needed to maintain SBP &gt; 90 mmHg
- Epinephrine 1:10,000 solution given IV at 1 \mug/minute initially, then 2-10 \mug/minute to maintain SBP &gt; 90 mmHg
- Norepinephrine 4 mg in 1 L 5% dextrose in water given IV at 2-12 \mug/min to maintain SBP &gt; 90 mmHg
- Glucagon 1 mg in 1 L 5% dextrose in water give IV at 5-15 \mug/minute for refractory hypotension
L2364: ## Bronchospasm
L2365: - Oxygen by face mask at 6-8 L/minute to maintain oxygen saturation at &gt; 90%
- Epinephrine 1:1000 solution, 0.5 mL subcutaneously
- Albuterol 0.5 mL of 0.5% solution in 2.5 mL of sterile saline every 15 minutes up to three doses or other inhaled beta agonists
- Corticosteroids
L2366: ## Laryngeal edema
L2367: - Epinephrine 1:1000 solution, 0.5 mL subcutaneously, repeat as needed every 5 to 10 minutes
- Corticosteroids
L2368: If symptoms resolve within a single study visit and the investigator determines the symptoms were not an occurrence of progressive or severe hypersensitivity, anaphylaxis, or anaphylactoid reactions then administration of the drug may continue according to the algorithm provided above, and at the discretion of the Investigator and Medical Director.

The biopsy data for 2 patients (out of the 16 patients enrolled in PB-102-F01/02) could not be used in the main efficacy analysis; 1 female patient had biopsy tissue that could not be scored at baseline as it was taken from the medulla of the kidney; 1 male patient had biopsy slides that were scanned out of focus and mislabeled, and subsequently could not be matched to correct visits (i.e. baseline versus six-month visit times could not be identified).

L2375: Timeline diagram illustrating the trial design for PB-102-F01, PB-102-F02, and PB-102-F03.
The timeline spans from Week 0 to Month 24.
PB-102-F01 (3 months):
Dose 0.2 mg/kg (Weeks 0-12)
Dose 1 mg/kg (Weeks 0-12)
Dose 2 mg/kg (Weeks 0-12)
PB-102-F02 (9 months):
Dose 0.2 mg/kg (Weeks 0-12)
Dose 1 mg/kg (Weeks 0-12)
Dose 2 mg/kg (Weeks 0-12)
PB-102-F01/F02 (12 months):
Dose 0.2 mg/kg (Weeks 0-12)
Dose 1 mg/kg (Weeks 0-12)
Dose 2 mg/kg (Weeks 0-12)
PB-102-F03 (Up to 60 months):
Dose 1 mg/kg (Weeks 0-12)
The main efficacy endpoint, Gb3 inclusion per kidney PTC, is assessed at baseline and at 6 months in Study PB-102-F01/F02. The supportive efficacy endpoint, change in Plasma Lyso-Gb3 is assessed at all time points shown in the figure. Study drug is administered intravenously every 2 weeks.

Figure 5 is a forest plot showing the absolute change in weighted mFSS score from Baseline to 6 months for various groups. The X-axis represents the Absolute Change in Weighted mFSS Score (95% CI), ranging from -2 to 0.5. A vertical dashed line is drawn at 0. All groups show a negative change (worsening score) compared to baseline, with the overall mean change being -0.8 (-1.1, -0.4). The plot includes data for Overall, Sex (Male, Female), Dose (0.2 mg/kg, 1 mg/kg, 2 mg/kg), and FD Type (Classic, Late-onset).
L2381: Figure 6 is a bar chart illustrating the overall distribution of the mFSS Score at Baseline (red bars) and 6 Months (teal bars). The X-axis represents the mFSS Score (0, 0.5, 1, 2, 3), and the Y-axis represents the Average Percentage of Capillaries (0 to 80). The chart shows that the distribution of mFSS scores shifted towards lower values (worsening scores) from Baseline to 6 Months. At Baseline, the highest percentage of capillaries had an mFSS score of 0 (approximately 35%). At 6 Months, the highest percentage of capillaries had an mFSS score of 0 (approximately 70%).

L2390: Flowchart illustrating the BLISS Scoring Procedure (Trial PB-102-F20).
The process begins with a Kidney bx slide, which is mailed to an imaging center. The slide is scanned using an Aperio ScanScope OS at 100X under oil immersion. The resulting images are processed by an Annotator. The Annotator then distributes the images to two Reader #1 and Reader #2, who perform the Scoring process on annotated images. The scores from both readers are sent to a Data analysis step. The flowchart also includes a citation: Barisani, Jennette, Colvin et al Archives Pathol Lab Med 2012.
Caption text below the flowchart:
Glass slides are mailed to an imaging center where they are scanned into virtual images. The annotator pathologist annotates PTC on WSI. Annotated WSI are triplicated and distributed to two reader-pathologists for scoring and one copy was maintained in the database at the image coordinating center. Abbreviation: bx, biopsy.
L2391: All patients
Inter-reader variability - Bland Altman Plot
Mean difference of readers 0.0002 (95% CI: -0.35, 0.35)
This Bland Altman plot displays the difference between Reader 1 and Reader 2 scores (Y-axis) versus the mean of the two readers (X-axis) for all patients, comparing Baseline (red circles) and Six-month (teal circles) time points.
The plot shows that the mean difference of readers is very close to zero (0.0002), and the 95% Confidence Interval (CI) for the mean difference is -0.35 to 0.35. The Upper Limit of Agreement and Lower Limit of Agreement are also shown, indicating that the differences between readers are generally small and within acceptable limits.
Time: Baseline (red circles), Six-month (teal circles).

L2394: Figure 16 displays 16 histograms arranged in a 4x4 grid, illustrating the permutation sampling distribution of the change in BLISS Score. Each histogram shows the distribution of "Actual change" (Y-axis, labeled "Proportion (%)") versus "Change score at six-month" (X-axis). The distributions are centered around zero, indicating no significant change under the null hypothesis. Red dashed lines mark the 2.5% and 97.5% quantiles, defining the critical region for a two-sided test. The label "(b) (6)" is present in the top left corner of each subplot.

L2399: Figure 20 displays four plots showing the estimated Glomerular Filtration Rate (eGFR) profile over time (Years since baseline visit) for subjects who prematurely discontinued the study (Trial PB-102-F20). The Y-axis represents eGFR (\text{mL}/\text{min}/1.73\text{m}^2), ranging from 50 to 100. The X-axis represents Years since baseline visit, ranging from -3 to 1. A vertical dashed line marks the time of premature discontinuation (approximately Year 0). The legend indicates two treatment groups: Fabrazyme (red line) and PRX-102 (teal line).
The plots are arranged in a 2x2 grid, showing results stratified by Sex (M/F) and UPCR (Urinary Protein-to-Creatinine Ratio) level (0.1, 0.06, 3.12, 0.11).

L2400: The figure displays two plots side-by-side.

Both curves show a rapid decrease in cumulative probability for low UPCR values, stabilizing around 0.05 probability for UPCR values greater than 1.5 gr/gr.

The overall correlation coefficient is reported as: Overall: r = -0.57 (p < 0.0001).

L2429: Figure 3 is a timeline diagram illustrating the trial design for PB-102-F01, PB-102-F02, and PB-102-F03.
The timeline spans from Week 0 to Month 24.
PB-102-F01 (3 months): Runs from Week 0 to Week 12. Dose: 0.2 mg/kg.
PB-102-F02 (9 months): Runs from Week 0 to Month 9. Dose: 1 mg/kg.
PB-102-F01/F02 (12 months): Runs from Week 0 to Month 12. Dose: 2 mg/kg.
PB-102-F03 (Up to 60 months): Runs from Month 12 onwards. Dose: 1 mg/kg.
The diagram shows that PB-102-F01, PB-102-F02, and PB-102-F01/F02 trials overlap during the first 12 months, while PB-102-F03 begins at Month 12.

Panel A: IgG Titer over Treatment Duration (months)

Patient (b) 0.2 mg/kg (Middle): IgG titer peaks around 4500 at 8 months, drops to 1000 at 10 months, and then rises to 2000 at 12 months before dropping to 0 at 14 months.
Patient (b) 1.0 mg/kg (Right): IgG titer remains near 0 across all time points.

Profiles are shown for Day 1 (blue), 3 Months (orange), 6 Months (gray), and 12 Months (yellow).
Patient (b) 0.2 mg/kg (Left): Concentration peaks around 1000 ng/mL at 24 hours for Day 1, 3 Months, and 6 Months, and around 1000 ng/mL at 48 hours for 12 Months.
Patient (b) 0.2 mg/kg (Middle): Concentration peaks around 1000 ng/mL at 24 hours for Day 1, 3 Months, and 6 Months, and around 1000 ng/mL at 48 hours for 12 Months.

The data points generally follow the identity line, indicating a good fit of the model predictions to the observed concentrations.

L2445: Figure 18 is a scatter plot showing Lysosomal \alpha-Galactosidase (Lys-Gb3) concentration (ng/mL) on the Y-axis versus PRX-102 AUC (g\cdothr/mL) on the X-axis, stratified by gender (Females and Males). The X-axis ranges from 0.0 to 3.0 g\cdothr/mL, and the Y-axis ranges from 0 to 250 ng/mL. A red smooth line is overlaid on the data points for both genders. For Females, Lys-Gb3 levels are generally low (below 50 ng/mL) and show a slight decrease as PRX-102 AUC increases. For Males, Lys-Gb3 levels are significantly higher, peaking around 200 ng/mL at low AUC values (0.0 to 1.0 g\cdothr/mL), and then decreasing sharply as PRX-102 AUC increases, stabilizing around 30-40 ng/mL at higher AUC values (2.0 to 3.0 g\cdothr/mL).
Note: The x-axis is the area under plasma drug concentration vs time curve (AUC) for a dose interval at steady-state
Source: Figure 12-7 of Applicant's PPK and PKPD report
L2446: Figure 19 is a scatter plot showing the percent change from baseline in BLISS Score on the Y-axis versus PRX-102 AUC (ng\cdothr/mL) on the X-axis, stratified by whether patients had Classic Fabry disease (Classic Fabry and otherwise). The X-axis ranges from 0 to 1,000,000 ng\cdothr/mL, and the Y-axis ranges from -100% to 20%. A red smooth line is overlaid on the data points for both groups. For Classic Fabry patients, the percent change from baseline in BLISS Score is generally negative, ranging from approximately -80% to -90%, and shows a slight decrease as PRX-102 AUC increases. For patients otherwise (non-Classic Fabry), the percent change from baseline in BLISS Score is generally positive, ranging from approximately -60% to 20%, and shows a positive, increasing trend as PRX-102 AUC increases.

L2451: Flowchart illustrating the BLISS Scoring Procedure:
Kidney bx slide is mailed to an imaging center.
Glass slides are scanned using Aperio ScanScope OS at 100X under oil immersion.
The Annotator pathologist annotates the WSI (Whole Slide Images).
Two identical copies of the annotated WSI are distributed to Reader #1 and Reader #2.
Reader #1 and Reader #2 perform the Scoring process on annotated images.
The data from the readers is used for Data analysis.
Barisoni, Jennette, Colvin et al
Archives Pathol Lab Med 2012
Glass slides are mailed to an imaging center where they are scanned into virtual images. The annotator pathologist annotates PTC on WSI. Annotated WSI are triplicated and distributed to two reader-pathologists for scoring and one copy was maintained in the database at the image coordinating center. Abbreviation: bx, biopsy.
